\"Portugal\") | 5,109 |\n\nRotterdam is diverse, with the demographics differing by neighbourhood. The city centre has a disproportionately high number of single people when compared to other cities, with 70% of the population between the ages of 20 and 40 identifying as single.[[39]](#cite_note-39) Those with higher education and higher income live disproportionately in the city centre, as do foreign-born citizens.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n### Composition\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=7 \"Edit section: Composition\")]\n\nSee also: [Districts and neighbourhoods of Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_and_neighbourhoods_of_Rotterdam \"Districts and neighbourhoods of Rotterdam\")\n\nThe [municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_the_Netherlands \"Municipalities of the Netherlands\") of Rotterdam is part of the [Rotterdam–The Hague metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam–The_Hague_metropolitan_area \"Rotterdam–The Hague metropolitan area\"), which, as of 2015, covers an area of 1,130 km2, of which 990 km km2 is land, and has a population of approximately 2,563,197. As of 2019, the municipality itself occupies an area of 325.79 km2, 208.80 km2 of which is land, and is home to 638,751 inhabitants.[[40]](#cite_note-40) Its population peaked at 731,564 in 1965, but the dual processes of [suburbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suburbanization \"Suburbanization\") and [counterurbanization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counterurbanization \"Counterurbanization\") saw this number steadily decline over the next 2 decades, reaching 560,000 by 1985.[[41]](#cite_note-41)[[42]](#cite_note-Pijbes_2016-42) Although Rotterdam has experienced population growth since then, it has done so at a slower pace than comparable cities in the Netherlands, like Amsterdam, The Hague and Utrecht.[[42]](#cite_note-Pijbes_2016-42)\n\nRotterdam consists of 14 submunicipalities: [Centrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Centrum \"Rotterdam Centrum\"), [Charlois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlois \"Charlois\") (including Heijplaat), [Delfshaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delfshaven \"Delfshaven\"), [Feijenoord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feijenoord \"Feijenoord\"), [Hillegersberg-Schiebroek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillegersberg-Schiebroek \"Hillegersberg-Schiebroek\"), [Hook of Holland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hook_of_Holland \"Hook of Holland\"), [Hoogvliet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoogvliet \"Hoogvliet\"), [IJsselmonde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IJsselmonde,_Rotterdam \"IJsselmonde, Rotterdam\"), [Kralingen-Crooswijk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen-Crooswijk \"Kralingen-Crooswijk\"), [Noord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noord,_Rotterdam \"Noord, Rotterdam\"), [Overschie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overschie \"Overschie\"), [Pernis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pernis,_Netherlands \"Pernis, Netherlands\"), and [Prins Alexander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prins_Alexander \"Prins Alexander\") (the most populous submunicipality with around 85,000 inhabitants). One other area, [Rozenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozenburg \"Rozenburg\"), has had an official submunicipality status since 18 March 2010. Since the status of a [submunicipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deelgemeente \"Deelgemeente\") was lifted on 19 March 2014, it has become an integral part of the municipality of Rotterdam.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nThe size of the municipality of Rotterdam is the result of the amalgamation of the following former municipalities,[[43]](#cite_note-43) some of which were [submunicipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deelgemeente \"Deelgemeente\") prior to 19 March 2014:\n\n* [Delfshaven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delfshaven \"Delfshaven\") (added on 30 January 1886)\n* [Charlois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlois \"Charlois\") (added on 28 February 1895)\n* [Kralingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen \"Kralingen\") (added on 28 February 1895)\n* [Hoogvliet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoogvliet \"Hoogvliet\") (added on 1 May 1934)\n* [Pernis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pernis,_Netherlands \"Pernis, Netherlands\") (added on 1 May 1934)\n* [Hillegersberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillegersberg \"Hillegersberg\") (added on 1 August 1941)\n* [IJsselmonde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IJsselmonde,_Rotterdam \"IJsselmonde, Rotterdam\") (added on 1 August 1941)\n* [Overschie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overschie \"Overschie\") (added on 1 August 1941)\n* [Schiebroek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schiebroek \"Schiebroek\") (added on 1 August 1941)\n* [Rozenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rozenburg \"Rozenburg\") (added on 18 March 2010)\n\n### Origin background makeup\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=8 \"Edit section: Origin background makeup\")]\n\nIn the Netherlands, Rotterdam has the highest percentage of residents with a recent [migration background](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_background \"Migration background\") from non-industrialised nations. They form a large part of Rotterdam's multi-ethnic and multicultural diversity. 52.9% of the population have at least one parent born outside the country. There are 80,000 Muslims, constituting 13% of the population in 2010.[[44]](#cite_note-44) The former mayor of Rotterdam, [Ahmed Aboutaleb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_Aboutaleb \"Ahmed Aboutaleb\") (2009–2024), is of Moroccan descent and is a practicing Muslim.[[45]](#cite_note-45)[[46]](#cite_note-46) The city is home to the largest Dutch Antillean community in the Netherlands.[[47]](#cite_note-47) The city also has its own [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown \"Chinatown\") at the West-Kruiskade, close to Rotterdam Centraal.[[48]](#cite_note-48)\n\nOrigin\n\n| Background groups | 1996[[49]](#cite_note-CBS_Statline-49) | 2000[[49]](#cite_note-CBS_Statline-49) | 2005[[49]](#cite_note-CBS_Statline-49) | 592,673 | 100% | 596,407 | 100% | 593,049 | 100% | 623,652 | 100% | 651,157 | 100% |\n\n### Religion\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=9 \"Edit section: Religion\")]\n\nReligions in Rotterdam (2013)[[50]](#cite_note-50)\n\n1. [Irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion \"Irreligion\") (46.7%)\n2. [Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church \"Catholic Church\") (18.7%)\n3. [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_Netherlands \"Islam in the Netherlands\") (13.1%)\n4. [Protestant Church in the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_Church_in_the_Netherlands \"Protestant Church in the Netherlands\") (10.5%)\n5. Other Christian denominations (7.10%)\n6. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism \"Hinduism\") (3.30%)\n7. [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism \"Buddhism\") (0.50%)\n8. [Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism \"Judaism\") (0.10%)\n\n[Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity \"Christianity\") is the largest religion in Rotterdam, with 36.3% of the population identifying. The second- and third-largest religions are [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam \"Islam\") (13.1%) and [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism \"Hinduism\") (3.3%), while about half of the population has [no religious affiliation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion \"Irreligion\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nSince 1795 Rotterdam has hosted the chief congregation of the liberal Protestant brotherhood of [Remonstrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remonstrants \"Remonstrants\"). From 1955 it has been the seat of the [Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church \"Catholic Church\") [bishop of Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bishop_of_Rotterdam \"Bishop of Rotterdam\") when the Rotterdam diocese was split from the [Haarlem diocese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Haarlem-Amsterdam \"Roman Catholic Diocese of Haarlem-Amsterdam\"). Since 2010 the city has been home to the largest mosque in the Netherlands, the [Essalam Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essalam_Mosque?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Essalam Mosque (page does not exist)\") [[nl](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Essalammoskee \"nl:Essalammoskee\")] (capacity 1,500).\n\n## Politics\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=10 \"Edit section: Politics\")]\n\nFurther information: [Government of Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Rotterdam \"Government of Rotterdam\")\n\nThe municipal council consists of 45 members, the largest party is [Livable Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livable_Rotterdam \"Livable Rotterdam\"). The municipal executive consists of mayor [Carola Schouten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carola_Schouten \"Carola Schouten\") and nine elderman, belonging to four parties.\n\n## Economy\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=11 \"Edit section: Economy\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/Nationale_Nederlanden%2C_Rotterdam.jpg/250px-Nationale_Nederlanden%2C_Rotterdam.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nationale_Nederlanden,_Rotterdam.jpg)\n\n[Gebouw Delftse Poort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebouw_Delftse_Poort \"Gebouw Delftse Poort\"), one of the tallest office buildings in the Netherlands\n\nRotterdam has always been one of the main centres of the shipping industry in the Netherlands. From the Rotterdam Chamber of the [VOC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company \"Dutch East India Company\"), the world's first multinational, established in 1602, to the merchant shipping leader Royal [Nedlloyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nedlloyd \"Nedlloyd\"), established in 1970, with its corporate headquarters located in the landmark building the 'Willemswerf' in 1988.[[51]](#cite_note-51) In 1997, Nedlloyd merged with the British shipping industry leader P&O, forming the third largest merchant shipping company in the world. The Anglo-Dutch [P&O Nedlloyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P&O_Nedlloyd \"P&O Nedlloyd\") was bought by the Danish giant corporation 'AP Moller [Maersk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._P._Moller-Maersk_Group \"A. P. Moller-Maersk Group\")' in 2005, and its Dutch operations are still headquartered in the 'Willemswerf'. Nowadays, well-known companies with headquarters in Rotterdam are consumer goods company [Unilever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unilever \"Unilever\") (since 2020 London), asset management firm [Robeco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robeco \"Robeco\"), energy company [Eneco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eneco_Energie \"Eneco Energie\"), dredging company [Van Oord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Oord \"Van Oord\"), oil company [Royal Dutch Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_plc \"Shell plc\") (since 2021 [London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/London \"London\")), terminal operator [Vopak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vopak \"Vopak\"), commodity trading company [Vitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitol \"Vitol\") and architecture firms [MVRDV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MVRDV \"MVRDV\") and [Office for Metropolitan Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_for_Metropolitan_Architecture \"Office for Metropolitan Architecture\").[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nIt is also home to the regional headquarters of chemical company [LyondellBasell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LyondellBasell \"LyondellBasell\"), commodities trading company [Glencore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glencore \"Glencore\"), pharmaceutical company [Pfizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pfizer \"Pfizer\"), logistics companies [Stolt-Nielsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stolt-Nielsen \"Stolt-Nielsen\"), electrical equipment company [ABB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABB \"ABB\") and consumer goods company [Procter & Gamble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procter_&_Gamble \"Procter & Gamble\"). Furthermore, Rotterdam has the Dutch headquarters of [Allianz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz \"Allianz\"), [Maersk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maersk \"Maersk\"), [Petrobras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrobras \"Petrobras\"), [Samskip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samskip?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Samskip (page does not exist)\"), [Louis Dreyfus Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Dreyfus_Group \"Louis Dreyfus Group\"), and [Aon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aon_(company) \"Aon (company)\"). The City of Rotterdam makes use of the services of semi-government companies [Roteb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roteb \"Roteb\") (to take care of [sanitation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanitation \"Sanitation\"), [waste management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waste_management \"Waste management\") and assorted services) and the Port of Rotterdam Authority (to maintain the [Port of Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Rotterdam \"Port of Rotterdam\")). Both these companies were once municipal bodies; now they are autonomous entities, owned by the city.\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/50/9-028_Rotterdam_ECT.jpg/250px-9-028_Rotterdam_ECT.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:9-028_Rotterdam_ECT.jpg)\n\nUnmanned vehicles handle containers at Europe Container Terminals (ECT), the largest container terminal operator in Europe.\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Waalhaven_in_Rotterdam_2016.jpg/250px-Waalhaven_in_Rotterdam_2016.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Waalhaven_in_Rotterdam_2016.jpg)\n\nThe Waalhaven\n\nBeing the largest port and one of the largest cities of the country, Rotterdam attracts many people seeking jobs, especially in the cheap labour segment. The city's unemployment rate is 12%, almost twice the national average.[[52]](#cite_note-52) Rotterdam is the largest [port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port \"Port\") in [Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Europe \"Europe\"), with the rivers [Maas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meuse_(river) \"Meuse (river)\") and [Rhine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhine \"Rhine\") providing excellent access to the [hinterland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinterland \"Hinterland\") upstream, reaching to [Basel, Switzerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basel \"Basel\"), and into France. In 2004 [Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai \"Shanghai\") took over as the world's [busiest container port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_container_ports \"List of busiest container ports\"). In 2006, Rotterdam was the world's seventh largest [container port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Containerization \"Containerization\") in terms of [twenty-foot equivalent units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit \"Twenty-foot equivalent unit\") (TEU) handled.[[53]](#cite_note-53) The port's main activities are [petrochemical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrochemical \"Petrochemical\") industries and general [cargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cargo \"Cargo\") handling and [transshipment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transshipment \"Transshipment\"). The harbour functions as an important transit point for [bulk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulk_material_handling \"Bulk material handling\") materials between the European continent and overseas. From Rotterdam, goods are transported by ship, river barge, train or road. In 2007, the *[Betuweroute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betuweroute \"Betuweroute\")*, a new fast freight [railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport \"Rail transport\") from Rotterdam to [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany \"Germany\"), was completed.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] the port also allows cruiseships to dock[[54]](#cite_note-54).\n\nWell-known streets in Rotterdam are the [Lijnbaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lijnbaan \"Lijnbaan\") (the first set of [pedestrian streets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedestrian_mall \"Pedestrian mall\") of the country, opened in 1953), the Hoogstraat, the [Coolsingel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coolsingel \"Coolsingel\") with the city hall, which was renovated between 2018 and 2021 giving cyclists and pedestrians more space, meaning that car traffic was reduced from 4 lanes (2 in each direction) to 2 lanes (1 in each direction). Another main street is the [Weena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weena_(Rotterdam) \"Weena (Rotterdam)\"), which runs from the Central Station to the [Hofplein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hofplein \"Hofplein\") (square). A modern shopping venue is the Beurstraverse (\"Stock Exchange Traverse\"), better known by its informal name '[Koopgoot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koopgoot?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Koopgoot (page does not exist)\")' ('Buying/Shopping Gutter', after its subterranean position), which crosses the Coolsingel below street level. The Kruiskade is a more upscale shopping street, with retailers like [Michael Kors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Kors \"Michael Kors\"), [7 For All Mankind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/7_For_All_Mankind \"7 For All Mankind\"), [Calvin Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvin_Klein \"Calvin Klein\"), [Hugo Boss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Boss \"Hugo Boss\"), [Tommy Hilfiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tommy_Hilfiger \"Tommy Hilfiger\") and the Dutch men's clothier [Oger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oger_(clothing_retailer)?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Oger (clothing retailer) (page does not exist)\"). Another upscale shopping venue is a flagship store of department store [De Bijenkorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Bijenkorf \"De Bijenkorf\"). Located a little more to the east is the [Markthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_hall_(Rotterdam) \"Market hall (Rotterdam)\"), with lots of small retailers inside. This hall is also one of Rotterdam's famous architectural landmarks. The main shopping venue in the south of Rotterdam is Zuidplein, which lies close to [Rotterdam Ahoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Ahoy \"Rotterdam Ahoy\"), an accommodation centre for shows, exhibitions, sporting events, concerts and congresses. Another prominent shopping centre called Alexandrium lies in the east of Rotterdam. It includes a large kitchen and furniture centre.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n## Education\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=12 \"Edit section: Education\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Rotterdam_standbeeld_Erasmus.jpg/250px-Rotterdam_standbeeld_Erasmus.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rotterdam_standbeeld_Erasmus.jpg)\n\nBronze statue of [Erasmus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus \"Erasmus\"), created by [Hendrick de Keyser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendrick_de_Keyser \"Hendrick de Keyser\") in 1622\n\nRotterdam has one major university, the [Erasmus University Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus_University_Rotterdam \"Erasmus University Rotterdam\") (EUR), named after one of the city's famous former inhabitants, [Desiderius Erasmus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desiderius_Erasmus \"Desiderius Erasmus\"). The Woudestein campus houses (among others) the [Rotterdam School of Management, Erasmus University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_School_of_Management,_Erasmus_University \"Rotterdam School of Management, Erasmus University\"). In the [Financial Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_Times \"Financial Times\")' 2005 rankings, it placed 29th globally and 7th in Europe. In the 2009 rankings of Masters of Management, the school reached first place with the [CEMS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Alliance_in_Management_Education \"Global Alliance in Management Education\") Master in Management and tenth place with its RSM Master in Management.[[55]](#cite_note-55) The university is also home to Europe's largest student association, STAR Study Association Rotterdam School of Management, Erasmus University, and the world's largest student association, [AIESEC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AIESEC \"AIESEC\"), has its international office in the city.\n\nThe Hoboken campus of EUR houses the Dijkzigt (general) hospital, the Sophia Hospital (for children), the [Daniel den Hoed clinic](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel%20den%20Hoedkliniek \"nl:Daniel den Hoedkliniek\") (cancer institute) and the medical department of the university. They are known collectively as the [Erasmus Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus_MC \"Erasmus MC\"). This centre is ranked third in Europe by CSIC[[56]](#cite_note-56) as a hospital and is also ranked within top 50 universities of the world in the field of medicine (clinical, pre-clinical & health, 2017).[[57]](#cite_note-57)\n\nThe [Willem de Kooning Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willem_de_Kooning_Academy \"Willem de Kooning Academy\") is Rotterdam's main art school, which is part of the Hogeschool Rotterdam. It is regarded as one of the most prestigious art schools in the Netherlands and the number 1 in Advertising and Copywriting. Part of the Willem de Kooning Academy is the [Piet Zwart Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piet_Zwart_Institute \"Piet Zwart Institute\") for postgraduate studies and research in Fine Art, Media Design and Retail Design. The Piet Zwart Institute boasts a selective roster of emerging international artists.\n\nThree *[hogescholen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_university \"Vocational university\")* (universities of applied sciences) exist in Rotterdam. These schools award their students a professional [bachelor's degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor's_degree \"Bachelor's degree\") and a postgraduate or [master's degree](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master's_degree \"Master's degree\"). The three *Hogescholen* are [Hogeschool Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hogeschool_Rotterdam \"Hogeschool Rotterdam\"), [Hogeschool Inholland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inholland_University_of_Applied_Sciences \"Inholland University of Applied Sciences\") and [Codarts University for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codarts \"Codarts\") (Codarts hogeschool voor de kunsten), a vocational university that teaches music, dance and circus.\n\nUnique to the city is the [Shipping & Transport College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/STC_Group \"STC Group\"), which offers master's, bachelor's and vocational diplomas at all levels.\n\n## Culture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=13 \"Edit section: Culture\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/HerdenkingVuurgrensRotterdam1940_2007_edit1.jpg/250px-HerdenkingVuurgrensRotterdam1940_2007_edit1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:HerdenkingVuurgrensRotterdam1940_2007_edit1.jpg)\n\nRotterdam waterfront, with spotlights shining into the air to commemorate the [Rotterdam Blitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Blitz \"Rotterdam Blitz\")\n\nOnce primarily a city of labour for its harbour and related industries, Rotterdam has developed into a city of culture, with various museums, cultural centres and activities, offering a stage for [architecture](#Architecture), music, visual arts, poetry, cinema, theatre, and culture more generally, with a range of festivals and other events and a buzzing nightlife. The city has its own orchestra, the [Rotterdam Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Philharmonic_Orchestra \"Rotterdam Philharmonic Orchestra\"), directed by [Lahav Shani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lahav_Shani \"Lahav Shani\"), which plays at the concert venue and convention center *[De Doelen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Doelen \"De Doelen\")*.[[58]](#cite_note-58) The [Ahoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Ahoy \"Rotterdam Ahoy\") complex in the south of the city is used for pop concerts, exhibitions, tennis tournaments and other activities. There are also several theatres and cinemas, including [LantarenVenster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Orleans_(Rotterdam) \"New Orleans (Rotterdam)\") and [Cinerama.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinerama_(Rotterdam) \"Cinerama (Rotterdam)\")[[59]](#cite_note-59)\n\nAlongside [Porto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porto \"Porto\"), Rotterdam was [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture \"European Capital of Culture\") in 2001.\n\n### Museums, libraries and archives\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=14 \"Edit section: Museums, libraries and archives\")]\n\nRotterdam has various cultural institutions. Well-known [museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum \"Museum\") are the [Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Boijmans_Van_Beuningen \"Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen\"), [Het Nieuwe Instituut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Het_Nieuwe_Instituut \"Het Nieuwe Instituut\"), the [Wereldmuseum Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wereldmuseum_Rotterdam \"Wereldmuseum Rotterdam\"), the [Kunsthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunsthal \"Kunsthal\"), [Kunstinstituut Melly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunstinstituut_Melly \"Kunstinstituut Melly\")[[60]](#cite_note-60) and the [Maritime Museum Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Museum_Rotterdam \"Maritime Museum Rotterdam\").[[61]](#cite_note-61) The Historical Museum Rotterdam has changed into [Museum Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Rotterdam \"Museum Rotterdam\"), which aims to exhibit the development of Rotterdam as a contemporary transnational city, and not merely the city's past.[[62]](#cite_note-62) Other museums include the [Tax & Customs Museum](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belasting%20&%20Douane%20Museum \"nl:Belasting & Douane Museum\"), the [Netherlands Marine Corps Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Marine_Corps_Museum \"Netherlands Marine Corps Museum\"), the [Rotterdam Public Transport Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Public_Transport_Museum \"Rotterdam Public Transport Museum\") and the [Natural History Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_History_Museum_Rotterdam \"Natural History Museum Rotterdam\"). The [FENIX Museum of Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FENIX_Museum_of_Migration \"FENIX Museum of Migration\") opened in May 2025.[[63]](#cite_note-63)\n\nThe first municipal library of Rotterdam was founded in 1604. The current [Bibliotheek Rotterdam](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bibliotheek%20Rotterdam \"nl:Bibliotheek Rotterdam\") (public library) was established in 1869 and is currently the largest cultural organization in Rotterdam, with fifteen branches across the city.\n\nThe Rotterdam City Archives (*Stadsarchief Rotterdam*) was established in 1857. Here one can find administrative records and sources about the city's historical development.[[64]](#cite_note-64) The archival holdings include, among others, general archives, notarial deeds, an audiovisual collection, and a library.\n\n### Popular music, film, festivals\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=15 \"Edit section: Popular music, film, festivals\")]\n\nRotterdam has a long tradition of popular music, including the city's jazz scene before and after WWII.[[65]](#cite_note-65) A major historical moment in the city's music history is the legendary [Kralingen Pop Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen_Music_Festival \"Kralingen Music Festival\"), which took place in Rotterdam in 1970 (featuring, among others, [Pink Floyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pink_Floyd \"Pink Floyd\"), [Jefferson Airplane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_Airplane \"Jefferson Airplane\"), [The Byrds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Byrds \"The Byrds\"), [Canned Heat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canned_Heat \"Canned Heat\"), [It's a Beautiful Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/It's_a_Beautiful_Day \"It's a Beautiful Day\"), and [Santana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santana_(band) \"Santana (band)\")). The festival was also made into a film, *Stamping Ground* (dir. George Sluizer).[[66]](#cite_note-66) Alternative (music) culture became prominently present in the city in these days. From the 1960s until the 2000s, Rotterdam had a thriving [squatters' movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squatting \"Squatting\"), which not only accommodated thousands of people but also created social centres and cultural venues.[[67]](#cite_note-67) From this movement came clubs like Boogjes, Eksit, Nighttown, Vlerk and Waterfront. A major reference was [Poortgebouw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poortgebouw \"Poortgebouw\"), which was squatted in 1980 and quickly legalised.\n\nRotterdam also became the home of [Gabber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabber \"Gabber\"), a type of hardcore electronic music popular in the mid-1990s, with hard beats and samples. Groups like [Neophyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neophyte_(band) \"Neophyte (band)\") and [Rotterdam Terror Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Terror_Corps \"Rotterdam Terror Corps\") (RTC) started in Rotterdam, playing at clubs like Parkzicht. In the years 2005–2011, the city struggled with keeping venues for pop music; many of them suffered severe financial problems.[[68]](#cite_note-68) This resulted in the disappearance of the major music venues Nighttown and WATT and smaller stages such as Waterfront, Exit, and Heidegger. The city today has a few stages for [pop music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pop_music \"Pop music\"), like [Rotown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotown \"Rotown\"), [Poortgebouw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poortgebouw \"Poortgebouw\") and Annabel. Additionally, the venue [WORM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WORM_(Rotterdam) \"WORM (Rotterdam)\") focuses on [experimental music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Experimental_music \"Experimental music\"), as well as various other cultural activities.\n\nWORM also screens films and hosts a film lab, *Filmwerkplaats*.[[69]](#cite_note-69) In fact, Rotterdam has an extensive film history,[[70]](#cite_note-70) ranging from avant-garde classics, such as [The Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_brug \"De brug\") ([Ivens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joris_Ivens \"Joris Ivens\"), 1928), to internationally acclaimed documentaries from the post-war era, such as [Steady!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houen_zo! \"Houen zo!\") (Van der Horst, 1952), and all kinds of fiction films. Of major importance within this context has also been the [International Film Festival Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Film_Festival_Rotterdam \"International Film Festival Rotterdam\") (IFFR), an annual event that lasts more than ten days (end of January, beginning of February), which has been organized since 1972. Besides the IFFR, several smaller film festivals take place in Rotterdam too, such as the [Architecture Film Festival Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_Film_Festival_Rotterdam \"Architecture Film Festival Rotterdam\") (AFFR).\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Flickr_-_FaceMePLS_-_Zomercarnaval_Rotterdam_2010_%2820%29.jpg/250px-Flickr_-_FaceMePLS_-_Zomercarnaval_Rotterdam_2010_%2820%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flickr_-_FaceMePLS_-_Zomercarnaval_Rotterdam_2010_(20).jpg)\n\n[Summer Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Carnival \"Summer Carnival\") in Rotterdam\n\nThroughout the year, many different festivals take place in Rotterdam. There are the summer festivals celebrating the city's multicultural population and identity, such as the Caribbean-inspired \"[Summer Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Carnival \"Summer Carnival\")\", the Dance Parade, Rotterdam 666, and the Metropolis pop festival. There are also [Poetry International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poetry_International_Web \"Poetry International Web\") (in June), the [North Sea Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sea_Jazz_Festival \"North Sea Jazz Festival\") (in July), the Valery Gergiev Festival (in September), and, also in September, the festival 'September in Rotterdam', the festival 'World of the Witte de With Quaret', and the [World Port Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Port_Days \"World Port Days\").\n\n### Eurovision Song Contest\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=16 \"Edit section: Eurovision Song Contest\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/ESC_2021_Rotterdam_-_Artwork_at_Ahoy_Parking.jpg/250px-ESC_2021_Rotterdam_-_Artwork_at_Ahoy_Parking.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ESC_2021_Rotterdam_-_Artwork_at_Ahoy_Parking.jpg)\n\nCity decor for the [Eurovision Song Contest 2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2021 \"Eurovision Song Contest 2021\")\n\nOn 30 August 2019, it was announced by the [European Broadcasting Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Broadcasting_Union \"European Broadcasting Union\") and Dutch television broadcasters [AVROTROS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AVROTROS \"AVROTROS\"), [NOS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nederlandse_Omroep_Stichting \"Nederlandse Omroep Stichting\") and [NPO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nederlandse_Publieke_Omroep_(organization) \"Nederlandse Publieke Omroep (organization)\") that Rotterdam would host the [Eurovision Song Contest 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2020 \"Eurovision Song Contest 2020\"), following the Dutch victory at the [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2019 \"Eurovision Song Contest 2019\") contest in [Tel Aviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv \"Tel Aviv\"), [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel \"Israel\"), with the song \"[Arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcade_(song) \"Arcade (song)\")\", performed by [Duncan Laurence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duncan_Laurence \"Duncan Laurence\").[[71]](#cite_note-71) However, due to the [COVID-19 pandemic in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Europe \"COVID-19 pandemic in Europe\"), the 2020 contest was cancelled, and Rotterdam was later retained as host of the [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_2021 \"Eurovision Song Contest 2021\") contest.[[72]](#cite_note-72)[[73]](#cite_note-73) The contest took place at [Rotterdam Ahoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Ahoy \"Rotterdam Ahoy\"), with the semi-finals taking place on 18 and 20 May 2021 and the final taking place on 22 May 2021.[[74]](#cite_note-74) This was the first time that Rotterdam hosted the contest and the first time that the Netherlands hosted the contest since [1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurovision_Song_Contest_1980 \"Eurovision Song Contest 1980\"), when it was held in [The Hague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hague \"The Hague\").\n\n### Rivalry\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=17 \"Edit section: Rivalry\")]\n\nThere is a healthy competition with [Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam \"Amsterdam\"), which is often viewed as the cultural capital of the Netherlands. This rivalry is most common amongst the city's football supporters, [Feyenoord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feyenoord \"Feyenoord\") (Rotterdam) and [Ajax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Ajax \"AFC Ajax\") (Amsterdam), and it is so severe that football games are only played with the home crowd present due to out-of-control riots in the past. There is a saying: \"Amsterdam to party, Den Haag (The Hague) to live, Rotterdam to work\". Another one, more popular with Rotterdammers, is \"Money is earned in Rotterdam, distributed in The Hague and spent in Amsterdam\".[[75]](#cite_note-75) Another saying that reflects both the rivalry between Rotterdam and Amsterdam is \"Amsterdam has it, Rotterdam doesn't need it\". Bright magazine editor Erwin van der Zande notes that this phrase is on T-shirts in Rotterdam.[[76]](#cite_note-76)\n\n## Architecture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=18 \"Edit section: Architecture\")]\n\nSee also: [List of tallest buildings in Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Rotterdam \"List of tallest buildings in Rotterdam\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Kop_van_Zuid_DSCF5860.jpg/500px-Kop_van_Zuid_DSCF5860.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kop_van_Zuid_DSCF5860.jpg)\n\nThe Wilhelmina pier at the [Kop van Zuid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kop_van_Zuid \"Kop van Zuid\") in the distance, on the left the [Erasmus Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmus_Bridge \"Erasmus Bridge\") can be seen\n\nRotterdam has become world famous because of its modern and groundbreaking architecture. Throughout the years the city has been nicknamed *[Manhattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manhattan \"Manhattan\") at the Meuse*[[77]](#cite_note-77)[[78]](#cite_note-78)[[79]](#cite_note-79)[[80]](#cite_note-80)[[81]](#cite_note-81)[[82]](#cite_note-82) and *The architectural capital of the Netherlands*[[83]](#cite_note-83)[[84]](#cite_note-84)[[85]](#cite_note-85) both for its skyline and because it is home to internationally leading architectural firms involved in the design of famous buildings and bridges in other big cities. Examples include [OMA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_for_Metropolitan_Architecture \"Office for Metropolitan Architecture\") ([Rem Koolhaas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rem_Koolhaas \"Rem Koolhaas\")), [MVRDV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MVRDV \"MVRDV\"), [Neutelings & Riedijk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutelings_Riedijk_Architects \"Neutelings Riedijk Architects\") and [Erick van Egeraat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erick_van_Egeraat \"Erick van Egeraat\").[[86]](#cite_note-86)[[87]](#cite_note-87) It has the reputation of being a platform for architectural development and education through the NAi ([Netherlands Architecture Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Architecture_Institute \"Netherlands Architecture Institute\")), which is open to the public and has a variety of exhibitions on architecture and urban planning issues, and previously the [Berlage Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlage_Institute \"Berlage Institute\"), a postgraduate laboratory of architecture. The city has 38 skyscrapers and 352 high-rises and has many skyscrapers planned or under construction.[[88]](#cite_note-88)[[89]](#cite_note-89) The top 5 highest buildings in the Netherlands consist entirely of buildings in Rotterdam.[[90]](#cite_note-90) It is home to the two tallest buildings in the Netherlands, the [Maastoren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maastoren \"Maastoren\") with a height of 165 meters and the [Zalmhaven Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Zalmhaven \"De Zalmhaven\") (completed in 2021) with a height of 215 meters.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\n### History\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=19 \"Edit section: History\")]\n\nIn 1898, the 45 m (148 ft) high-rise office building the White House (in Dutch [Witte Huis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witte_Huis \"Witte Huis\")) was completed, at that time the tallest office building in Europe. In the first decades of the 20th century, some influential architecture in the modern style was built in Rotterdam. Notable are the [Van Nelle fabriek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Nelle_Factory \"Van Nelle Factory\") (1929) a monument of modern factory design by [Brinkman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannes_Brinkman \"Johannes Brinkman\") and [Van der Vlugt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leendert_van_der_Vlugt \"Leendert van der Vlugt\"), the [Jugendstil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Nouveau \"Art Nouveau\") clubhouse of the [Royal Maas Yacht Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Maas_Yacht_Club \"Royal Maas Yacht Club\") designed by Hooijkaas jr. en Brinkman (1909), and [Feyenoord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feyenoord \"Feyenoord\")'s football stadium [De Kuip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Kuip \"De Kuip\") (1936), also by Brinkman and Van der Vlugt. The architect [J. J. P. Oud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobus_Oud \"Jacobus Oud\") was a famous Rotterdammer in those days. The Van Nelle Factory obtained the status of UNESCO [World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site \"World Heritage Site\") in 2014. During the early stages of [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II \"World War II\"), the centre of Rotterdam was [bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Blitz \"Rotterdam Blitz\") by the German [Luftwaffe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luftwaffe \"Luftwaffe\"), destroying many of the older buildings in the centre of the city. After an initial crisis of reconstruction, the centre of Rotterdam has become the site of the ambitious new architecture.[[91]](#cite_note-91)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Kubuswoningen_in_rotterdam.jpg/250px-Kubuswoningen_in_rotterdam.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kubuswoningen_in_rotterdam.jpg)\n\nThe [Cube Houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cube_house \"Cube house\"), popularly known as the *Blaak-forest* in 2014\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Markthal_Rotterdam_bij_avond.jpg/250px-Markthal_Rotterdam_bij_avond.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Markthal_Rotterdam_bij_avond.jpg)\n\nThe [Markthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_hall_(Rotterdam) \"Market hall (Rotterdam)\") at night as seen from the Binnenrotte\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/Euromast2.jpg/250px-Euromast2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Euromast2.jpg)\n\nThe [Euromast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromast \"Euromast\") in 2005\n\nRotterdam is also famous for its [Lijnbaan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lijnbaan \"Lijnbaan\") 1952 by architects Broek en Bakema, Peperklip by architect Carel Weeber, and the Kubuswoningen or [cube houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cube_house \"Cube house\") designed by architect [Piet Blom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piet_Blom \"Piet Blom\") in 1984.\n\nThe newest landmark in Rotterdam is the [Markthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_hall_(Rotterdam) \"Market hall (Rotterdam)\"), designed by architect firm [MVRDV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MVRDV \"MVRDV\"). In addition to that, there are many internationally well-known architects based in Rotterdam, like [O.M.A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_for_Metropolitan_Architecture \"Office for Metropolitan Architecture\") ([Rem Koolhaas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rem_Koolhaas \"Rem Koolhaas\")), [Neutelings & Riedijk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutelings_Riedijk_Architects \"Neutelings Riedijk Architects\") and [Erick van Egeraat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erick_van_Egeraat \"Erick van Egeraat\"), to name a few. Two architectural landmarks are located in the Lloydkwartier: the STC college building and the [Schiecentrale 4b](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schiecentrale \"Schiecentrale\"). The construction of the Depot of the [Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Boijmans_Van_Beuningen \"Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen\") was started in 2003 and was officially opened by King Willem-Alexander on 5 November 2021. It is the world's first fully accessible art depot.[[92]](#cite_note-92)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/Erasmusbrug_across_the_Nieuwe_Maas_river.jpg/250px-Erasmusbrug_across_the_Nieuwe_Maas_river.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Erasmusbrug_across_the_Nieuwe_Maas_river.jpg)\n\n[Erasmus Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmusbrug \"Erasmusbrug\") in 2011\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Montevideo_2010.JPG/250px-Montevideo_2010.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Montevideo_2010.JPG)\n\nThe former headquarters of the [Holland America Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holland_America_Line \"Holland America Line\") next to modern residential architecture in 2010\n\nRotterdam also houses several of the tallest structures in the Netherlands.\n\n* The [Erasmusbrug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmusbrug \"Erasmusbrug\") (1996) is a 790-meter (2,600 ft) cable-stayed bridge linking the north and south of Rotterdam. It is held up by a 138 m (453 ft) tall pylon with a characteristic bend, earning the bridge its nickname 'De Zwaan' ('the Swan').\n* Rotterdam has the tallest residential building in the Netherlands: the [De Zalmhaven Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Zalmhaven \"De Zalmhaven\") (215 m (705.4 ft)).\n* Rotterdam is also home to the tallest office building in the Netherlands, the '[Maastoren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maastoren \"Maastoren\")' (164.75 m or 540.5 ft), which houses [Deloitte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deloitte \"Deloitte\"). This office tower surpassed the 'Delftse Poort' (160 m or 520 ft), which houses the Nationale-Nederlanden insurance company, part of [ING Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ING_Group \"ING Group\"), as the tallest office tower in 2009.[[93]](#cite_note-93)[[94]](#cite_note-94)\n* The skyline of Rotterdam also houses the 185 m (607 ft) tall [Euromast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromast \"Euromast\"), which is a major [tourist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism \"Tourism\") attraction. It was built in 1960, initially reaching a height of 101 m (331 ft); in 1970, the Euromast was extended by 85 m (279 ft).\n\nRotterdam has a reputation for being a platform for architectural development and education through the Berlage Institute, a postgraduate laboratory of architecture, and the NAi ([Netherlands Architecture Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_Architecture_Institute \"Netherlands Architecture Institute\")), which is open to the public and has a variety of exhibitions on architecture and urban planning issues.\n\nOver 30 new highrise projects are being developed. A Guardian journalist wrote in 2013 that \"All this is the consequence of the city suffering a bombardment of two things: bombs and architects.\"[[31]](#cite_note-Wainwright_2013-31)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Rotterdam_Panorama_Euromast_5_July_2019.jpg/1920px-Rotterdam_Panorama_Euromast_5_July_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rotterdam_Panorama_Euromast_5_July_2019.jpg)\n\n### Parks\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=20 \"Edit section: Parks\")]\n\nWithin Rotterdam's urban structure, parks and greenery play an important role. A number of well-known parks in Rotterdam are:\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Arboretum_Trompenburg_Nl_2.jpg/250px-Arboretum_Trompenburg_Nl_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Arboretum_Trompenburg_Nl_2.jpg)\n\nArboretum Trompenburg\n\n* *Het Park* ([Het Park bij de Euromast](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Het%20Park%20(Rotterdam) \"nl:Het Park (Rotterdam)\")) is a 70-acre park on the Maas, south of the Westzeedijk, at the [Euromast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromast \"Euromast\"). The eastern half of the park was constructed between 1852 and 1863 to a design by the firm Jan David Zocher. The western part was added in 1866 with some modifications. The first [Floriade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Floriade_(Netherlands) \"Floriade (Netherlands)\") in 1960 was held in Het Park, with the Euromast observation tower being erected to mark the event. [National Heritage site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rijksmonument \"Rijksmonument\") since 2011. Originally, the park continued across the Westzeedijk, where the Medical Faculty was built in the 1960s (now Erasmus MC). See also: Museumpark.\n* [Museumpark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museumpark \"Museumpark\"), close to Het Park, was originally designed in 1927 by architect W.G. Witteveen, who also designed [Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_Boijmans_Van_Beuningen \"Museum Boijmans Van Beuningen\"). Today, several museums have clustered around the park.\n* [Diergaarde Blijdorp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diergaarde_Blijdorp \"Diergaarde Blijdorp\"), which is situated on the northwest side of Rotterdam, complete with a walkthrough sea aquarium called the [Oceanium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanium \"Oceanium\").\n* [Arboretum Trompenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arboretum_Trompenburg \"Arboretum Trompenburg\") in [Kralingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen \"Kralingen\"). The park dates back to 1820, but it was only after it was opened to the public in 1958 that the park, which was managed by the (Van Hoey) Smith family for generations, gained wider attention. The park, approximately 20 acres in size, contains approximately 4,000 different types of trees, shrubs and perennials, including the national plant collections of [conifers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conifers \"Conifers\"), *[Quercus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quercus \"Quercus\")*, *[Fagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beech \"Beech\")*, *[Rhododendron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhododendron \"Rhododendron\")*, *[Ligustrum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ligustrum \"Ligustrum\")*, *[Rodgersia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodgersia \"Rodgersia\")* and *[Hosta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosta \"Hosta\")*, among others.\n* *Park Schoonoord* (3 acres) is located in the [Scheepvaartkwartier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scheepvaartkwartier \"Scheepvaartkwartier\") and was designed in its current form in 1860 by Jan David Zocher.\n* The [*Kralingse Bos*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen-Crooswijk \"Kralingen-Crooswijk\") (500 acres) with the [Kralingse Plas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingse_Plas \"Kralingse Plas\") (250 acres) is located in the [Kralingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen \"Kralingen\") district and has been based on a design by [Marinus Jan Granpré Molière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marinus_Jan_Granpré_Molière \"Marinus Jan Granpré Molière\") since 1928. In 1953 the Kralingse Bos officially opened.[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1c/20070619Rozenburgpark2.jpg/250px-20070619Rozenburgpark2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:20070619Rozenburgpark2.jpg)\n\n  Park Rozenburg\n* The *Vroesenpark* in the district Rotterdam-Noord was laid out from 1929 to a plan by city architect W.G. [Witteveen](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willem%20Gerrit%20Witteveen \"nl:Willem Gerrit Witteveen\").\n* The *Zuiderpark* (780 acres) is located in the district of [Charlois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlois \"Charlois\"). The park was laid out as a utility park from 1952 and not as an ornamental park.\n* *Park Rozenburg* is a 7.41 acres (3.00 ha) park in the neighbourhood of [Kralingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kralingen \"Kralingen\"). The park is a protected municipal monument (Dutch: *Gemeentelijk monument*).[[95]](#cite_note-95)[[96]](#cite_note-96)\n\n#### Green activities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=21 \"Edit section: Green activities\")]\n\nSince 28 May 1994, Rotterdam has had the phenomenon [*Opzoomeren*](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/opzoomeren \"wiktionary:opzoomeren\"). 15% of Rotterdam residents (about 100,000 residents) say they participate in this phenomenon.[[97]](#cite_note-97) At the end of 2020, the city has a record number of 2,503 Opzoomer streets,[[98]](#cite_note-98) which is mainly reflected in the construction of facade gardens.\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Dakpark_Rotterdam.jpg/250px-Dakpark_Rotterdam.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Dakpark_Rotterdam.jpg)\n\nDakpark Rotterdam\n\nThe municipality of Rotterdam is encouraging the construction of [green roofs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_roof \"Green roof\"). There is an attractive subsidy for roof owners, and the city has now provided a number of municipal buildings with a green roof. As of 1 January 2020, the water storage capacity requirement has been increased to 30 liters of water storage capacity per square metre. This reduces the burden on the sewer system during heavy rainfall and reduces the risk of flooding on the street.[[99]](#cite_note-99)\n\n* The city's largest green roof is located on top of the [Groothandelsgebouw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groothandelsgebouw \"Groothandelsgebouw\") next to Central Station.\n* The *Dakakker* is the largest roof farm in Europe on top of the Schieblok.\n* The *Dakpark* is an elongated, narrow park in the district [Bospolder-Tussendijken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bospolder-Tussendijken \"Bospolder-Tussendijken\") in Rotterdam-West. It has been built at a height of about nine meters, is about 85 meters wide and extends for about a kilometer from Hudson Square to near Marconi Square.[[100]](#cite_note-100)\n* The municipality of Rotterdam will provide the flat roof of the conference and concert building [De Doelen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Doelen \"De Doelen\") with greenery and water storage. The design for the roof was made by Kraijvanger Architects.[[101]](#cite_note-101)\n\n## Sports\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=22 \"Edit section: Sports\")]\n\nRotterdam calls itself *Sportstad* (City of Sports). The city annually organises several world-renowned sporting events. Some examples are the [Rotterdam Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Marathon \"Rotterdam Marathon\"), the [World Port Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Port_Tournament \"World Port Tournament\"), and the [Rotterdam World Tennis Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Open \"Rotterdam Open\"). Rotterdam has also hosted a race of the [Red Bull Air Race World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Air_Race_World_Championship \"Red Bull Air Race World Championship\") and the car racing event [Monaco aan de Maas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monaco_aan_de_Maas \"Monaco aan de Maas\") (Monaco at the Meuse).\n\nThe city is also the home of many sports clubs and some historic and iconic athletes.\n\n### Football\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=23 \"Edit section: Football\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Robin_Van_Persie_August_2007.jpg/250px-Robin_Van_Persie_August_2007.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Robin_Van_Persie_August_2007.jpg)\n\n[Robin van Persie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_van_Persie \"Robin van Persie\") began his career with [Excelsior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excelsior_Rotterdam \"Excelsior Rotterdam\") and broke through in Feyenoord.\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bc/Rotterdam_feyenoord_stadion_1.jpg/250px-Rotterdam_feyenoord_stadion_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rotterdam_feyenoord_stadion_1.jpg)\n\nDe Kuip, Feyenoord home stadium\n\nRotterdam is the home of three professional [football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football \"Association football\") clubs, being [first-tier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eredivisie \"Eredivisie\") clubs [Feyenoord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feyenoord \"Feyenoord\"), [Sparta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sparta_Rotterdam \"Sparta Rotterdam\"), and [Excelsior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Excelsior_Rotterdam \"Excelsior Rotterdam\").\n\nFeyenoord, founded in 1908 and the dominant of the three professional clubs, has won sixteen national titles since the introduction of professional football in the Netherlands. It won the [UEFA Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Champions_League \"UEFA Champions League\") as the first Dutch club in 1970 and won the World Cup for club teams in the same year. In 1974, they were the first Dutch club to win the [UEFA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Europa_League \"UEFA Europa League\"), and in 2002, Feyenoord won the UEFA Cup again. In 2008, the year of their 100-year anniversary, Feyenoord won the [KNVB Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KNVB_Cup \"KNVB Cup\").\n\nSeating 51,480, its 1937 stadium, called *Stadion Feijenoord* but popularly known as [De Kuip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Kuip \"De Kuip\") ('the Tub'), is the second-largest in the country, after the [Amsterdam Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam_Arena \"Amsterdam Arena\"). De Kuip, located in the southeast of the city, has hosted many international football games, including the final of [Euro 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2000 \"UEFA Euro 2000\"), and has been awarded a FIFA 5-star ranking. There were concrete plans to build a new stadium with a capacity of at least 63,000 seats; these fell through after supporter backlash and lack of funds.\n\nSparta, founded in 1888 and situated in the northwest of Rotterdam, won the national title six times; Excelsior (founded 1902), in the northeast, has never won any.\n\nRotterdam also has three [fourth tier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoofdklasse \"Hoofdklasse\") clubs, SC Feijenoord (Feyenoord Amateurs), PVV DOTO and TOGR. Rotterdam is and has been the home to many great football players and coaches, among whom:\n\n* [Bert van Marwijk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bert_van_Marwijk \"Bert van Marwijk\")\n* [Coen Moulijn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coen_Moulijn \"Coen Moulijn\")\n* [Howard D Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_D_Carter?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Howard D Carter (page does not exist)\")\n* [Dirk Kuyt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dirk_Kuyt \"Dirk Kuyt\")\n* [Ernst Happel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernst_Happel \"Ernst Happel\")\n* [Faas Wilkes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faas_Wilkes \"Faas Wilkes\")\n* [Giovanni van Bronckhorst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_van_Bronckhorst \"Giovanni van Bronckhorst\")\n* [Georginio Wijnaldum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georginio_Wijnaldum \"Georginio Wijnaldum\")\n* [Henrik Larsson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henrik_Larsson \"Henrik Larsson\")\n* [Danny Blind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danny_Blind \"Danny Blind\")\n* [John de Wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_de_Wolf \"John de Wolf\")\n* [Jon Dahl Tomasson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jon_Dahl_Tomasson \"Jon Dahl Tomasson\")\n* [Leo Beenhakker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Beenhakker \"Leo Beenhakker\")\n* [Louis van Gaal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_van_Gaal \"Louis van Gaal\")\n* [Ove Kindvall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ove_Kindvall \"Ove Kindvall\")\n* [Kevin Strootman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Strootman \"Kevin Strootman\")\n* [Memphis Depay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Depay \"Memphis Depay\")\n* [Pierre van Hooijdonk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_van_Hooijdonk \"Pierre van Hooijdonk\")\n* [Pim Doesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pim_Doesburg \"Pim Doesburg\")\n* [Puck van Heel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puck_van_Heel \"Puck van Heel\")\n* [Rinus Israël](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rinus_Israël \"Rinus Israël\")\n* [Robin van Persie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robin_van_Persie \"Robin van Persie\")\n* [Ronald Koeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Koeman \"Ronald Koeman\")\n* [Roy Makaay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Makaay \"Roy Makaay\")\n* [Ruud Gullit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruud_Gullit \"Ruud Gullit\")\n* [Sonny Silooy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonny_Silooy \"Sonny Silooy\")\n* [Willem van Hanegem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willem_van_Hanegem \"Willem van Hanegem\")\n* [Wim Jansen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wim_Jansen \"Wim Jansen\")\n* [Winston Bogarde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Bogarde \"Winston Bogarde\")\n* [Włodzimierz Smolarek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Włodzimierz_Smolarek \"Włodzimierz Smolarek\")\n* [Julio Ricardo Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julio_Ricardo_Cruz \"Julio Ricardo Cruz\")\n\n### Marathon\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=24 \"Edit section: Marathon\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Hardlopers_marathon_in_Rotterdam.JPG/250px-Hardlopers_marathon_in_Rotterdam.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Hardlopers_marathon_in_Rotterdam.JPG)\n\nRunners during the marathon in Rotterdam\n\nRotterdam has its own annual international [marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Marathon \"Rotterdam Marathon\"), which offers one of the fastest courses in the world. From 1985 until 1998, the [world record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathon_world_record_progression \"Marathon world record progression\") was set in Rotterdam, first by [Carlos Lopes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Lopes \"Carlos Lopes\") and later in 1988 by [Belayneh Densamo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belayneh_Densamo \"Belayneh Densamo\").\n\nIn 1998, the world record for women was set by [Tegla Loroupe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tegla_Loroupe \"Tegla Loroupe\"), in a time of 2:20.47. Loroupe won the Rotterdam Marathon three consecutive times, from 1997 to 1999.\n\nThe track record for men is held by [Bashir Abdi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashir_Abdi \"Bashir Abdi\"), who ran a time of 2:03.36 in 2021. The female record was set in 2012 when [Tiki Gelana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiki_Gelana \"Tiki Gelana\") finished the race in 2:18.58. Gelana went on to become the 2012 Olympic champion in London a few months later.\n\nThe marathon starts and ends on the *[Coolsingel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coolsingel \"Coolsingel\")* in the heart of Rotterdam. It attracts a total of 900,000 visitors.\n\n### Tennis\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=25 \"Edit section: Tennis\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/ABN-wereldtennistoernooi_in_Rotterdam_Arthur_Ashe_in_actie%2C_Bestanddeelnr_927-7839.jpg/250px-ABN-wereldtennistoernooi_in_Rotterdam_Arthur_Ashe_in_actie%2C_Bestanddeelnr_927-7839.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Arthur_Ashe.jpg)\n\nArthur Ashe at the [1975 ABN World Tennis Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1975_ABN_World_Tennis_Tournament \"1975 ABN World Tennis Tournament\")\n\nSince 1972, Rotterdam has hosted the indoor hard court [ABN AMRO World Tennis Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Open \"Rotterdam Open\") (part of the [ATP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Tennis_Professionals \"Association of Tennis Professionals\") Tour) ever since the event was first organised in 1972, when it was won by [Arthur Ashe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Ashe \"Arthur Ashe\"). Ashe went on to win the tournament two more times, making him the singles title record holder.\n\nFormer [Wimbledon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Championships,_Wimbledon \"The Championships, Wimbledon\") winner [Richard Krajicek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Krajicek \"Richard Krajicek\") became the tournament director after his retirement in 2000. The latest edition of the tournament attracted a total of 116,354 visitors.[[102]](#cite_note-102)\n\n### Tour de France\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=26 \"Edit section: Tour de France\")]\n\nIn November 2008 Rotterdam was chosen as the host of the [Grand Départ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Départ \"Grand Départ\") of the [2010 Tour de France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Tour_de_France \"2010 Tour de France\"). Rotterdam won the selection over the Dutch city of [Utrecht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utrecht \"Utrecht\"). Germany's Düsseldorf had previously also expressed interest in hosting. The [Amaury Sport Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amaury_Sport_Organisation \"Amaury Sport Organisation\") (ASO), the organizer of the Tour de France, said in a statement on its website that it chose Rotterdam because, in addition to it being another big city, like London, to showcase the use of bikes for urban transportation, it provided a location well-positioned considering the rest of the route envisioned for the 2010 event. The start in Rotterdam was the fifth to take place in the Netherlands. The [prologue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prologue \"Prologue\") was a 7 km (4.35 mi) individual time trial crossing the centre of the city. The first regular stage left the [Erasmusbrug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erasmusbrug \"Erasmusbrug\") and went south, towards Brussels.[[103]](#cite_note-103)\n\nThe second stage of the [2015 edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Tour_de_France \"2015 Tour de France\") took the riders through Rotterdam on their way to [Neeltje Jans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neeltje_Jans \"Neeltje Jans\") in Zeeland.[[104]](#cite_note-104)[[105]](#cite_note-105)\n\nThe 2024 edition of the [Tour de France Femmes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tour_de_France_Femmes \"Tour de France Femmes\") began in Rotterdam, with three stages in the Netherlands.[[106]](#cite_note-106)\n\n### Rowing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=27 \"Edit section: Rowing\")]\n\nMembers of the student rowing club [Skadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skadi_(rowing_club) \"Skadi (rowing club)\") were part of the '[Holland Acht](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holland_Acht \"Holland Acht\")', winning a gold medal at the [Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowing_at_the_Summer_Olympics \"Rowing at the Summer Olympics\") in [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Summer_Olympics \"1996 Summer Olympics\").[[107]](#cite_note-107) Since the opening in April 2013, Rotterdam hosts the rowing venue [Willem-Alexander Baan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willem-Alexander_Baan \"Willem-Alexander Baan\") that hosted the [2016 World Rowing Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_World_Rowing_Championships \"2016 World Rowing Championships\") for Seniors, U23 and Juniors.\n\n### Field hockey\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=28 \"Edit section: Field hockey\")]\n\nIn [field hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_hockey \"Field hockey\"), Rotterdam has the largest hockey club in the Netherlands, [HC Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HC_Rotterdam \"HC Rotterdam\"), with its own stadium in the north of the city and nearly 2,400 members. The first men's and women's teams both play on the highest level in the Dutch *Hoofdklasse*.\n\n### Baseball\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=29 \"Edit section: Baseball\")]\n\nRotterdam is home to the most successful European [baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baseball \"Baseball\") team, [Neptunus Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DOOR_Neptunus \"DOOR Neptunus\"), winning the most [European Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Cup_(baseball) \"European Cup (baseball)\").\n\n### Boxing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=30 \"Edit section: Boxing\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Bep_van_Klaveren%2C_Olympische_Spelen_1928_Amsterdam.jpg/250px-Bep_van_Klaveren%2C_Olympische_Spelen_1928_Amsterdam.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bep_van_Klaveren,_Olympische_Spelen_1928_Amsterdam.jpg)\n\nBep van Klaveren\n\nRotterdam has a long boxing tradition starting with [Bep van Klaveren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bep_van_Klaveren \"Bep van Klaveren\") (1907–1992), aka 'The Dutch Windmill', Gold medal winner of the 1928 [Amsterdam Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amsterdam_Olympics \"Amsterdam Olympics\"), followed by professional boxers like [Regilio Tuur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regilio_Tuur \"Regilio Tuur\") and Don Diego Poeder.\n\n### Swimming\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=31 \"Edit section: Swimming\")]\n\nRotterdam's swimming tradition started with [Marie Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Braun \"Marie Braun\"), also known as Zus (sister) Braun, who was coached to a gold medal at the 1928 Amsterdam Olympics by her mother, Ma Braun, and 3 European titles three years later in Paris. In her career as a 14-time national champion, she broke 6 world records. Ma Braun later also coached the Rotterdam-born, three-times Olympic champion [Rie Mastenbroek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rie_Mastenbroek \"Rie Mastenbroek\") during the Berlin Olympics in 1936. In later years, [Inge de Bruijn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inge_de_Bruijn \"Inge de Bruijn\") became a Rotterdam sports icon as a triple Olympic gold medal winner in 2000 and a triple European gold medal winner in 2001.\n\n### Sailing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=32 \"Edit section: Sailing\")]\n\nOlympic gold medalist, in the [O-Jolle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O-Jolle \"O-Jolle\") during the [1936 Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sailing_at_the_1936_Summer_Olympics \"Sailing at the 1936 Summer Olympics\"), [Daan Kagchelland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daan_Kagchelland \"Daan Kagchelland\") was born in Rotterdam and was a member of the [Rotterdamsche Zeil Vereeniging](https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdamsche%20Zeil%20Vereeniging \"nl:Rotterdamsche Zeil Vereeniging\"). The Kralingse Plas was and is still a source of Olympic sailors like [Koos de Jong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koos_de_Jong \"Koos de Jong\"), [Ben Verhagen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Verhagen \"Ben Verhagen\"), [Henny Vegter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henny_Vegter \"Henny Vegter\"), [Serge Kats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serge_Kats \"Serge Kats\") and [Margriet Matthijsse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margriet_Matthijsse \"Margriet Matthijsse\").\n\n### Motorcycle racing\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=33 \"Edit section: Motorcycle racing\")]\n\nMotorcycle speedway was staged in the Feyenoord Stadium after the Second World War. The team which raced in a Dutch league was known as the Feyenoord Tigers. The team included Dutch riders and some English and Australian riders.\n\n### Sportsmen of the Year election\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=34 \"Edit section: Sportsmen of the Year election\")]\n\nSince 1986, the city has selected its best sportsman, woman and team at the [Rotterdam Sports Awards Election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Sportsman_of_the_year \"Rotterdam Sportsman of the year\"), held in December.\n\n### Other famous Rotterdam athletes\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=35 \"Edit section: Other famous Rotterdam athletes\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Francisco_Elson.jpg/250px-Francisco_Elson.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Francisco_Elson.jpg)\n\n[Francisco Elson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Elson \"Francisco Elson\")\n\n* [Mia Audina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mia_Audina \"Mia Audina\"), a retired Indonesia-born badminton player living in Rotterdam.\n* [Nelli Cooman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelli_Cooman \"Nelli Cooman\"), a Surinamese-born retired athlete who held the [60 m](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60_metres \"60 metres\") dash world record and was the world and European champion in that event.\n* [Robert Doornbos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Doornbos \"Robert Doornbos\"), a Rotterdam-born race car driver who competed in the [Formula One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One \"Formula One\").\n* [Robert Eenhoorn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Eenhoorn \"Robert Eenhoorn\"), a Rotterdam-born retired MLB shortstop, who competed for the [New York Yankees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Yankees \"New York Yankees\"), the [Anaheim Angels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaheim_Angels \"Anaheim Angels\") and the [New York Mets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Mets \"New York Mets\").\n* [Dex Elmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dex_Elmont \"Dex Elmont\"), a Rotterdam-born judoka, who finished second in the European championships in 2009 in the 65 to 73 kg (143 to 161 lb) division.\n* [Guillaume Elmont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillaume_Elmont \"Guillaume Elmont\"), a Rotterdam-born judoka, who became world champion in 2005 in the 73 to 81 kg (161 to 179 lb) division.\n* [Francisco Elson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Elson \"Francisco Elson\"), a Rotterdam-born basketball player who played in the NBA, won the NBA Finals in 2007 with the [San Antonio Spurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Antonio_Spurs \"San Antonio Spurs\").\n* [Ignisious Gaisah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignisious_Gaisah \"Ignisious Gaisah\"), a Ghanaian-born long jumper with a personal best of 8.43 m (27.66 ft), residing in Rotterdam since 2001. Gaisah is a multiple medal winner in several international events, both as a citizen of Ghana and the Netherlands.\n* [Francis Hoenselaar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Hoenselaar \"Francis Hoenselaar\"), a Rotterdam-born female darts player, generally recognised as the best Dutch female darts player ever.\n* [Robert Lathouwers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Lathouwers \"Robert Lathouwers\"), an athlete born in a Rotterdam suburb, specialised in the 800 m. Lathouwers gained international notoriety when he got disqualified after shoving Irish athlete [David McCarthy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_McCarthy_(born_1983) \"David McCarthy (born 1983)\") in the 2010 European Championships.\n* [Fatima Moreira de Melo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatima_Moreira_de_Melo \"Fatima Moreira de Melo\"), a Rotterdam-born, 2008 Olympic champion in field hockey. Moreira de Melo is a professional poker player.\n* [Piet Roozenburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piet_Roozenburg \"Piet Roozenburg\"), a Rotterdam-born draughts player, who was the world champion from 1948 to 1956 and the 8-time Dutch champion.\n* [Betty Stöve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betty_Stöve \"Betty Stöve\"), a Rotterdam-born retired female tennis doubles specialist and 10-time Grand Slam winner.\n* [Ingmar Vos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ingmar_Vos \"Ingmar Vos\"), a Rotterdam-born decathlete, with a personal best of 8224 points.\n\n## Yearly events\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=36 \"Edit section: Yearly events\")]\n\nRotterdam hosts several annual events unique to the city. It hosts the *Zomercarnaval* (Summer Carnaval), the second-largest Caribbean carnival in Europe, originally called the Antillean Carnival. Other events include the North Sea Jazz Festival, the largest jazz festival in Europe, and a three-day-long maritime extravaganza called the World Port Days, celebrating the Port of Rotterdam.\n\n* January:\n  + [Six Days of Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Six_Days_of_Rotterdam \"Six Days of Rotterdam\") – Cycling, Rotterdam Ahoy\n  + [International Film Festival Rotterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Film_Festival_Rotterdam \"International Film Festival Rotterdam\")[[108]](#cite_note-108)\n* February:\n  + [Rotterdam Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Open \"Rotterdam Open\") – Tennis, Rotterdam Ahoy\n  + Art Rotterdam – International art fair, Van Nellefabriek\n* April–June:\n  + [Rotterdam Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Marathon \"Rotterdam Marathon\")[[109]](#cite_note-109)\n  + [Poetry International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poetry_International_Web \"Poetry International Web\")\n  + [Koningsdag Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koningsdag \"Koningsdag\") (27 April)[[110]](#cite_note-110)\n  + CHIO (Concours Hippique International Officiel) Rotterdam[[111]](#cite_note-111)\n  + [Roparun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roparun \"Roparun\")\n* July:\n  + [North Sea Jazz Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sea_Jazz_Festival \"North Sea Jazz Festival\") (second weekend of July)\n  + [Summer Carnival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Carnival \"Summer Carnival\")[[112]](#cite_note-112)\n* August:\n  + Pleinbioscoop[[113]](#cite_note-113)\n  + Dag van de Romantische Muziek (Romantic music festival)\n  + [Rotterdam Rave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Rave \"Rotterdam Rave\") Festival\n* September:\n  + [The World Port Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Port_Days \"The World Port Days\")[[114]](#cite_note-114)\n* November/December\n  + [Sinterklaas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinterklaas \"Sinterklaas\") (last weekend before 5 December)\n  + Boterletterwedstrijden (sailing regatta for international classes)[[115]](#cite_note-115)\n\n## Transportation\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Rotterdam&action=edit&section=37 \"Edit section: Transportation\")]\n\nSee also: [Rotterdam Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Metro \"Rotterdam Metro\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Rtd_CS-III.JPG/250px-Rtd_CS-III.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Rtd_CS-III.JPG)\n\nRotterdam's new [Central Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotterdam_Centraal_railway_station \"Rotterdam Centraal railway station\") reopened in March 2014, designed to handle up to 320,000 passengers daily.\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Metro_%2832912087507%29.jpg/250px-Metro_%2832912087507%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Metro_(32912087507).jpg)\n\nA Bombardier 5700 series EMU between [Vlaardingen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vlaardingen \"Vlaardingen\") and [Hoek van Holland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hook_of_Holland \"Hook of
